# Complete supplied source

# Opinion brief and source

SYNTHETIC EVALUATION MATERIAL. Fictional policy debate, not legal advice or a real municipal process. Source language: Swedish.

## Brief

Skriv en debattartikel för invånarna i fiktiva Lervik. Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut. Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb. En tydlig, sakligt skarp text på ungefär 400 ord. Markdown med orienterande rubriker. Ingen kommersiell CTA.

## Underlag

Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler. Under försöket gjordes 96 bokningar via webb och 24 via telefon. Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.

Tjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni föreslår att telefonbokning tas bort för alla sju lokaler från september. Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden. Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna. Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.

Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar. Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb. Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket. Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till. Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.

Sanna Eks ståndpunkt: ”Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben. Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.” Detta får citeras eller refereras. Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna. Hon motsätter sig inte digital bokning.

## Boundaries / judging

Inga lagkrav, protester, diskrimineringsfall, partipolitiska motiv eller besparingar är belagda. Siffror ska tillskrivas rapporten där de bär argumentet. Författaren ska kunna kritisera beslutsunderlaget skarpt utan falska motiv eller tandlös neutralisering. Slutet ska namnge kommunstyrelsens möjliga beslut, med kostnadsosäkerheten bevarad.

# Delivered user response

Delivered with one known defect: “digital experience” broadens the source’s “digital vana.” The suggested repair is “digital familiarity.” The wording remains because the [write skill](/private/var/folders/cs/vgp_x_353zd9frkjpysp7zdw0000gn/T/kntnt-native-session-bgcn6qjf/work/.agents/skills/write/SKILL.md) says to “never reopen the prose after its final source comparison.”

Opinion in American English; no technique, as the genre specifies. Both comparisons completed. The trial’s cost remains unspecified. Temporary files were removed.

```markdown
---
kntnt:
  genre: opinion
  technique: none
  language: en_US
---

# Lervik should postpone digital-only venue booking

An eight-week pilot in two association venues cannot settle the trade-off for all seven. The municipal executive board needs evidence on staff workload and users’ reasons for choosing each booking route before permanently removing telephone bookings.

By Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut

The staff memorandum for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on June 18 proposes ending telephone bookings at all seven association venues from September. Öppna beslut calls for a six-month trial with telephone and web bookings available. A permanent change should wait until the board can weigh administrative costs against value to users.

## The pilot counts bookings, not residents

The municipality’s April 8, 2026, report, *Bokning av föreningslokaler*, records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial in two venues. Those figures describe booking activity.

They do not establish how many different people used either route. The report measures neither age, functional ability, nor digital experience. Turning the telephone total into a claim about residents unable to book online would misrepresent the evidence.

## The workload objection deserves a measured answer

The administration wants staff to avoid entering information through two workflows. That is a real objection to keeping both routes, and it deserves an answer. But the documents contain neither a measurement of staff time nor a calculation of financial savings.

The board should not treat an administrative rationale as a quantified benefit. Without those measurements, it cannot weigh staff costs against value to users.

## A six-month trial would test the trade-off

Our proposal covers all seven venues and retains telephone and web bookings. During the trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route and ask users to volunteer their reasons for choosing it.

I do not oppose digital booking. A telephone booking is not proof that someone cannot use the web; it is a reason to find out why the telephone is still used.

## The board must weigh the trial’s cost

Öppna beslut makes no claim to have funded the trial or estimated its cost. The municipal executive board should postpone the permanent switch, assess the trial’s still-uncertain cost, and authorize six months with both routes. It can then decide whether to remove, change, or retain a booking route.
```

# Lervik should postpone digital-only venue booking

An eight-week pilot in two association venues cannot settle the trade-off for all seven. The municipal executive board needs evidence on staff workload and users’ reasons for choosing each booking route before permanently removing telephone bookings.

By Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut

The staff memorandum for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on June 18 proposes ending telephone bookings at all seven association venues from September. Öppna beslut calls for a six-month trial with telephone and web bookings available. A permanent change should wait until the board can weigh administrative costs against value to users.

## The pilot counts bookings, not residents

The municipality’s April 8, 2026, report, *Bokning av föreningslokaler*, records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial in two venues. Those figures describe booking activity.

They do not establish how many different people used either route. The report measures neither age, functional ability, nor digital experience. Turning the telephone total into a claim about residents unable to book online would misrepresent the evidence.

## The workload objection deserves a measured answer

The administration wants staff to avoid entering information through two workflows. That is a real objection to keeping both routes, and it deserves an answer. But the documents contain neither a measurement of staff time nor a calculation of financial savings.

The board should not treat an administrative rationale as a quantified benefit. Without those measurements, it cannot weigh staff costs against value to users.

## A six-month trial would test the trade-off

Our proposal covers all seven venues and retains telephone and web bookings. During the trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route and ask users to volunteer their reasons for choosing it.

I do not oppose digital booking. A telephone booking is not proof that someone cannot use the web; it is a reason to find out why the telephone is still used.

## The board must weigh the trial’s cost

Öppna beslut makes no claim to have funded the trial or estimated its cost. The municipal executive board should postpone the permanent switch, assess the trial’s still-uncertain cost, and authorize six months with both routes. It can then decide whether to remove, change, or retain a booking route.

# Lervik should postpone web-only venue booking

An eight-week pilot in two association venues cannot settle the trade-off for all seven. The municipal executive board needs evidence on staff workload and users’ reasons for choosing each booking route before permanently removing telephone bookings.

By Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut

The staff memorandum for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on June 18 proposes ending telephone bookings at all seven association venues from September. Öppna beslut calls for a six-month trial with telephone and web bookings available. A permanent change should wait until the board can weigh administrative costs against value to users.

## The pilot counts bookings, not residents

The municipality’s April 8, 2026, report, *Bokning av föreningslokaler*, records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial in two venues. Those figures describe booking activity.

They do not establish how many different people used either route. The report measures neither age, functional ability, nor digital experience. Turning the telephone total into a claim about residents unable to book online would misrepresent the evidence.

## The workload objection deserves a measured answer

The administration wants staff to avoid entering information through two workflows. That is a real objection to keeping both routes, and it deserves an answer. But the documents contain neither a measurement of staff time nor a calculation of financial savings.

The board should not treat an administrative rationale as a quantified benefit. Without those measurements, it cannot weigh staff costs against value to users.

## A six-month trial would test the trade-off

Our proposal covers all seven venues and retains telephone and web bookings. During the trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route and ask users to volunteer their reasons for choosing it.

I do not oppose digital booking. A telephone booking is not proof that someone cannot use the web; it is a reason to find out why the telephone is still used.

## The board must weigh the trial’s cost

Öppna beslut makes no claim to have funded the trial or estimated its cost. The municipal executive board should postpone the permanent switch, assess the trial’s still-uncertain cost, and authorize six months with both routes. It can then decide whether to remove, change, or retain a booking route.

Referent identification was checked separately from person, number, and natural gender. No unsupported natural-gender assertion occurs.

## Cross-language and implication checks

The source-support comparison covers translations outside quotation marks as well as direct factual restatement. There are no translated quotations to review separately for quotation voice.

- **Digital/web booking:** The material identifies telephone and web as the two booking routes, describes ending telephone booking, and gives the thesis as a permanent switch to digital booking. Within this supplied proposal, the draft's references to a web-only switch identify the same described change. A separate app, SMS system, kiosk, or third booking route would be additional material absent from the proposal; the draft does not assert the existence or removal of such a route. No concrete contextual contradiction is established by the title.
- **Föreningslokaler / association venues:** The English term identifies places for association activities. It does not state that an association owns the premises, and the surrounding municipal context makes no new ownership claim. Both source and draft use two premises for the pilot and seven for the proposal.
- **Kommunstyrelsen / municipal executive board:** The English names the same municipality-level body throughout, without implying a national cabinet, elected mayor, or different municipal committee.
- **Tjänsteutlåtande / staff memorandum:** This is the staff document presented before the board meeting and stating the proposal and rationale. The draft makes no claim about its legal effect, signatory, internal confidentiality, or document-production procedure.
- **Digital vana / digital experience:** In the personal-characteristic list, “digital experience” denotes familiarity gained using digital tools. It is not read as a digital product's user experience or as a single interface event. The supplied lack of measurement is retained without claiming that the report established lack of familiarity.
- **Arbetskostnad / administrative costs / staff costs:** In context, the draft's administration-cost phrase refers to the staff work in the two workflows, with the later “staff costs” making that referent explicit. The draft does not claim a figure for overhead, software, buildings, or aggregate expenditure. The passage is advocacy about what the board should compare.
- **Bookings/residents/unique persons:** The heading's population-count contrast is clarified immediately as a failure to establish how many different people used each route. It does not turn bookings into residents, identify user residence, or assert that no users are residents. The source's no-unique-person-count statement supports the heading and body limitation.
- **Administrative rationale and benefits:** The draft preserves the actual stated rationale and confines the absence of measurement to the documents. It neither claims there are no savings nor invents motives about user laziness or expense. The author's reasoning calls for measurement rather than asserting a causal effect of either route.
- **Cost uncertainty and chronology:** The closing preserves the association's limited claim and the explicitly required cost uncertainty. It leaves the cost for the board and preserves possible removal, change, and retention. A trial is proposed before a permanent switch; neither its start date nor a later decision date is invented.

For each translated term or potentially stronger implication above, the ordinary contextual reading supplies the stated referent. No concrete case was found in which the draft asserts a different factual state while remaining compatible with the complete supplied material. Hypothetical extra channels, expenses, report types, or demographic counts would not, by their mere possibility, change what these passages assert in their supplied context. Conversely, the source's additional details are not silently converted into unsupported opposite claims when abbreviated by a heading or summary.

## Findings

Source-support findings: none.

Translation findings: none. No direct quotations are present; indirect expression of Sanna Ek's quoted standpoint preserves its meaning and first-person authorship.

No repairs are proposed because the draft is defensible as written. General editorial and mechanical preferences were not reviewed.

Completion status: complete. Unresolved findings: none.

## Cross-language and implication checks

The source-support comparison covers translations outside quotation marks as well as direct factual restatement. There are no translated quotations to review separately for quotation voice.

- **Digital/web booking:** The material identifies telephone and web in the pilot and proposed six-month trial, but gives the permanent-switch thesis as “enbart digital bokning.” The title changes that scope to “web-only.” A future system allowing web and an app but no telephone is a concrete case compatible with the material: it is digital-only, but is not web-only. The source's description of the temporary phone/web trial does not exclude additional digital routes in a permanent system. In the reverse direction, web-only booking is digital-only; no reverse counterexample was found. This one-way narrowing is source-support finding F1, outside quoted speech. |
- **Föreningslokaler / association venues:** The English term identifies places for association activities. It does not state that an association owns the premises, and the surrounding municipal context makes no new ownership claim. Both source and draft use two premises for the pilot and seven for the proposal.
- **Kommunstyrelsen / municipal executive board:** The English names the same municipality-level body throughout, without implying a national cabinet, elected mayor, or different municipal committee.
- **Tjänsteutlåtande / staff memorandum:** This is the staff document presented before the board meeting and stating the proposal and rationale. The draft makes no claim about its legal effect, signatory, internal confidentiality, or document-production procedure.
- **Digital vana / digital experience:** In the personal-characteristic list, “digital experience” denotes familiarity gained using digital tools. It is not read as a digital product's user experience or as a single interface event. The supplied lack of measurement is retained without claiming that the report established lack of familiarity.
- **Arbetskostnad / administrative costs / staff costs:** In context, the draft's administration-cost phrase refers to the staff work in the two workflows, with the later “staff costs” making that referent explicit. The draft does not claim a figure for overhead, software, buildings, or aggregate expenditure. The passage is advocacy about what the board should compare.
- **Bookings/residents/unique persons:** The heading's population-count contrast is clarified immediately as a failure to establish how many different people used each route. It does not turn bookings into residents, identify user residence, or assert that no users are residents. The source's no-unique-person-count statement supports the heading and body limitation.
- **Administrative rationale and benefits:** The draft preserves the actual stated rationale and confines the absence of measurement to the documents. It neither claims there are no savings nor invents motives about user laziness or expense. The author's reasoning calls for measurement rather than asserting a causal effect of either route.
- **Cost uncertainty and chronology:** The closing preserves the association's limited claim and the explicitly required cost uncertainty. It leaves the cost for the board and preserves possible removal, change, and retention. A trial is proposed before a permanent switch; neither its start date nor a later decision date is invented.

For the terms and implications other than digital/web above, the ordinary contextual reading supplies the stated referent; no concrete case was found in which the draft asserts a different factual state while remaining compatible with the complete supplied material. For F1, the app-and-web case remains a finding even though the supplied material neither describes it nor makes it likely. The source contains no statement excluding such a permanent digital arrangement. Source details abbreviated by other headings or summaries are not silently converted into unsupported opposite claims.

## Findings

### F1 — Title narrows the permanent proposal from digital-only to web-only

Draft: “Lervik should postpone web-only venue booking.”

Source: “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.” The operative staff proposal is: “Tjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni föreslår att telefonbokning tas bort för alla sju lokaler från september.”

Changed support: The source names a permanent digital-only arrangement and telephone removal. It does not establish a permanent web-exclusive arrangement. The pilot and temporary trial naming phone and web do not settle what every future digital booking route could be.

Both-direction test: A permanent system accepting bookings through a website and a separate app, with telephone booking removed, meets “enbart digital” but fails “web-only.” That case is compatible with the supplied statements: none restricts a permanent digital system to the website. In the reverse direction, web-only booking is necessarily digital-only, so no case was found where the draft's exclusivity holds but the source's digital exclusivity fails. The changed term is therefore narrower in one direction.

Smallest supported repair: Change the title to “Lervik should postpone digital-only venue booking.” Keep the supplied phone/web wording where it describes the pilot or proposed trial.

Source-support findings: one, F1.

Translation findings: none. No direct quotations are present; indirect expression of Sanna Ek's quoted standpoint preserves its meaning and first-person authorship.

Only the title repair above is proposed. General editorial and mechanical preferences were not reviewed.

Completion status: complete. Unresolved findings: F1 remains in the supplied draft; the checker has not altered the draft.

# Writer validation of comparison 1

The complete report was read and its claim accounting checked against the complete draft and source. It covers the headline, standfirst, byline, lead, each section heading, every body paragraph, the ending, and pronoun features. No additional accounting mismatch or unresolved claim was detected.

F1 accepted. The draft headline said “Lervik should postpone web-only venue booking.” The source thesis says “kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler”. The source does not confine a future permanent digital arrangement to the web. The supported repair is to replace only “web-only” with “digital-only” in the headline. This repair has been applied, preserving all other prose. A fresh comparison of the complete current draft and original source is required.

# Captured file version: scratch/write-source-check-lm0do59d/draft.md

# Lervik should postpone digital-only venue booking

An eight-week pilot in two association venues cannot settle the trade-off for all seven. The municipal executive board needs evidence on staff workload and users’ reasons for choosing each booking route before permanently removing telephone bookings.

By Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut

The staff memorandum for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on June 18 proposes ending telephone bookings at all seven association venues from September. Öppna beslut calls for a six-month trial with telephone and web bookings available. A permanent change should wait until the board can weigh administrative costs against value to users.

## The pilot counts bookings, not residents

The municipality’s April 8, 2026, report, *Bokning av föreningslokaler*, records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial in two venues. Those figures describe booking activity.

They do not establish how many different people used either route. The report measures neither age, functional ability, nor digital experience. Turning the telephone total into a claim about residents unable to book online would misrepresent the evidence.

## The workload objection deserves a measured answer

The administration wants staff to avoid entering information through two workflows. That is a real objection to keeping both routes, and it deserves an answer. But the documents contain neither a measurement of staff time nor a calculation of financial savings.

The board should not treat an administrative rationale as a quantified benefit. Without those measurements, it cannot weigh staff costs against value to users.

## A six-month trial would test the trade-off

Our proposal covers all seven venues and retains telephone and web bookings. During the trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route and ask users to volunteer their reasons for choosing it.

I do not oppose digital booking. A telephone booking is not proof that someone cannot use the web; it is a reason to find out why the telephone is still used.

## The board must weigh the trial’s cost

Öppna beslut makes no claim to have funded the trial or estimated its cost. The municipal executive board should postpone the permanent switch, assess the trial’s still-uncertain cost, and authorize six months with both routes. It can then decide whether to remove, change, or retain a booking route.

# Source comparison report, pass 2

Compared the complete draft, including its title, summary, headings, byline, body, and closing implications, with the complete supplied `source.md`. The genre is opinion, the target language is en_US, and the author is Sanna Ek. No external material was used. The draft contains no direct quotations. The draft and source were not changed.

## Complete claim accounting

### 1. Title: requested postponement and the proposed booking change

Draft: “Lervik should postpone digital-only venue booking.”

Source: “Skriv en debattartikel för invånarna i fiktiva Lervik. Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut. Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.”

Assessment: Supported advocacy. “Lervik” names the locality whose municipal executive board is the decision maker; the body makes that subject explicit. “Digital-only” preserves “enbart digital.” “Venue” is an ordinary English description of the identified association premises in this booking context. The title does not claim that digital booking itself should cease: “digital-only” identifies the proposal to be postponed. The title omits “permanent,” but its postponement request does not alter the permanent-change claim explained immediately below it.

### 2. Summary: pilot duration, venue count, and full scope

Draft: “An eight-week pilot in two association venues cannot settle the trade-off for all seven.”

Source: “Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler.” Also: “Tjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni föreslår att telefonbokning tas bort för alla sju lokaler från september.” And: “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.”

Assessment: The duration, two-venue scope, and seven-venue decision scope are supported. “Cannot settle the trade-off” is the author's argument about the evidential limits of the supplied pilot and documents, not a newly invented pilot result. It is grounded in the missing workload and financial measurements discussed in the body. The source does not establish how representative the two venues are, and the draft does not claim that they are unrepresentative.

### 3. Summary: information sought and order of decision

Draft: “The municipal executive board needs evidence on staff workload and users’ reasons for choosing each booking route before permanently removing telephone bookings.”

Source: “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.” Also: “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.” And: “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”

Assessment: Supported standpoint and proposal. “Workload” is used here to identify the information sought, not as a claim that workload was measured or increased. The order—get evidence before the permanent removal—is the brief's own thesis. There is no claim that users must disclose reasons; the body retains their voluntary participation.

### 4. Byline: identity and representative role

Draft: “By Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut.”

Source: “Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut.”

Assessment: Same person, role, and organization. “Spokesperson” is gender-neutral and does not add a natural-gender assertion. The source separately uses “Hon,” but the draft does not need to repeat that information. Omitting the organization's legal/type description “föreningen” in the byline does not invent or change its identity.

### 5. Opening: document, meeting, proposal, scope, and commencement

Draft: “The staff memorandum for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on June 18 proposes ending telephone bookings at all seven association venues from September.”

Source: “Tjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni föreslår att telefonbokning tas bort för alla sju lokaler från september.” Context: “Skriv en debattartikel för invånarna i fiktiva Lervik.”

Assessment: Same document and decision body, meeting date, proposal modality, all-seven scope, booking route, and September commencement. The draft says the document proposes the change; it does not present the proposal as a resolution already passed. “Staff memorandum” describes the identified municipal staff statement without adding a claim about its authors, issuance process, or authority. “Association venues” refers to the same premises introduced by the source's “föreningslokaler.”

### 6. Opening: association's proposed trial

Draft: “Öppna beslut calls for a six-month trial with telephone and web bookings available.”

Source: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.” Also: “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.”

Assessment: Same organization, proposal modality, duration, and two routes. The all-seven qualification is carried in the later statement of the proposal; this sentence does not assert a different scope.

### 7. Opening: weighing costs against value

Draft: “A permanent change should wait until the board can weigh administrative costs against value to users.”

Source: “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.” Also: “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”

Assessment: Supported advocacy. The source names labor cost, while “administrative costs” is a broader expression for the subject of the author's proposed assessment. Read in context with the following discussion of staff entering data in two workflows and the later explicit “staff costs,” this does not claim that any additional category of administrative expense has been documented. It is not a factual assertion that a cost measurement exists. The postponement, assessment, and user-value standpoint are preserved.

### 8. First heading: unit counted

Draft: “The pilot counts bookings, not residents.”

Source: “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer.” Also: “Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.”

Assessment: Supported contrast in this article's resident-facing context. The report counts booking events; it does not provide a resident count. The body immediately specifies different people, so the heading does not change the unit into a count of total residents or imply that booking users must all be residents.

### 9. Report attribution, title, and date

Draft: “The municipality’s April 8, 2026, report, *Bokning av föreningslokaler* …”

Source: “Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026 …”

Assessment: Same issuing source, date, and title. The numbers are explicitly attributed to the report where they carry the argument.

### 10. Reported counts, routes, duration, and setting

Draft: “… records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial in two venues.”

Source: “Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler. Under försöket gjordes 96 bokningar via webb och 24 via telefon.”

Assessment: Same counts, booking-event unit, routes, eight-week period, and two-premises scope. The draft supplies no percentage, comparative valuation, unique-user count, causal effect, or unsupported extrapolation.

### 11. What the figures describe

Draft: “Those figures describe booking activity.”

Source: “Under försöket gjordes 96 bokningar via webb och 24 via telefon. Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer.”

Assessment: Supported description of the counted unit. “Those” refers to the immediately preceding two counts; it adds no demographic information.

### 12. Unknown number of people

Draft: “They do not establish how many different people used either route.”

Source: “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer.”

Assessment: Preserves the limitation of the counts. It does not claim that nobody used both routes, that the people are all different, or that there were no identifiable people. “They” refers to the figures, so its plurality does not assert anything about people or natural gender. “Different people” corresponds to unique persons. No particular unique-user number is invented.

### 13. Unmeasured age

Draft: “The report measures neither age …”

Source: “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.” The referent of “Den” is established by the preceding sentence: “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer.”

Assessment: Same report, negative measurement claim, and variable. The negation applies across all three coordinated variables in both languages. No age group or causal relationship to route choice is inferred.

### 14. Unmeasured functional ability

Draft: “… functional ability …”

Source: “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.”

Assessment: “Functional ability” expresses “funktionsförmåga” without narrowing it to a named disability, physical ability only, or a technical skill. The draft retains the report-level absence of measurement and adds no disability prevalence or exclusion claim.

### 15. Unmeasured digital variable

Draft: “… nor digital experience.”

Source: “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.”

Assessment: Source-support finding F1 below. “Digital experience” can name accumulated exposure or experience, whereas “digital vana” names accustomedness or familiarity with digital use. The negative measurement statement is not safely interchangeable between those variables.

### 16. Limits of telephone-count inference

Draft: “Turning the telephone total into a claim about residents unable to book online would misrepresent the evidence.”

Source: “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.” Also: “Sanna Eks ståndpunkt: ‘Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben. Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.’ Detta får citeras eller refereras.”

Assessment: Supported criticism of an inference drawn from the booking count. It neither denies that some residents could be unable to book online nor asserts that every telephone user is able to do so. Its broader wording about an inability claim is supported by Sanna's supplied position that an individual telephone booking is not proof of inability, alongside the report's population-inference limitation.

### 17. Workload heading

Draft: “The workload objection deserves a measured answer.”

Source: “Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden. Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna. Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.”

Assessment: The objection's existence and subject are supported. “Deserves” is the author's evaluation; “measured answer” is advocacy for the measurement supplied in the proposal. No workload amount or already demonstrated benefit is claimed.

### 18. Administration's rationale

Draft: “The administration wants staff to avoid entering information through two workflows.”

Source: “Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden.” Also: “Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.”

Assessment: Same administration, staff activity, two-flow rationale, and desired avoidance. The source explicitly establishes the administration's actual objection, so “wants” does not manufacture an unstated motive. “Through” does not assert identical data-entry software or duplication of every field.

### 19. Reality of the objection and judgment on it

Draft: “That is a real objection to keeping both routes, and it deserves an answer.”

Source: “Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.” Also: “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg …”

Assessment: “Real” corresponds to the source's explicit “verkliga,” not an unsupported valuation of the size of the objection. “Deserves an answer” is the author's judgment grounded in the supplied objection and proposal. “That” refers to the preceding rationale, without any natural-gender content.

### 20. Absence of time and savings calculations in the documents

Draft: “But the documents contain neither a measurement of staff time nor a calculation of financial savings.”

Source: “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.” Context: “Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden.”

Assessment: Same documents, absence claim, and two measures. “Staff time” identifies the subject established by the preceding staff-work rationale. The scope remains the documents; it does not claim that no person has measured time anywhere or that no savings could occur.

### 21. Rationale versus quantified benefit

Draft: “The board should not treat an administrative rationale as a quantified benefit.”

Source: “Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden. Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.”

Assessment: Supported editorial argument from the distinction between a rationale and absent quantification. It does not assert that the board has already made this mistake or that the administrative rationale has no value.

### 22. Staff cost and user value

Draft: “Without those measurements, it cannot weigh staff costs against value to users.”

Source: “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.” Also: “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”

Assessment: Supported expression of Sanna's reasoning and standpoint. It claims no staff-cost number and no amount of user value. “Those measurements” refers to the measurements absent from the documents; “it” refers to the board. The latter pronoun is singular, organizational, and does not convey natural gender. “Staff costs” corresponds to labor costs in this staff-administration context.

### 23. Trial heading and prospective test

Draft: “A six-month trial would test the trade-off.”

Source: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar. Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.” Also: “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”

Assessment: Supported prospective argument. “Would test” identifies the purpose of the proposed evidence collection; it does not guarantee a successful result, measured saving, or answer favoring either route. The duration remains six months.

### 24. Scope, two routes, and first-person ownership

Draft: “Our proposal covers all seven venues and retains telephone and web bookings.”

Source: “Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut.” Also: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.” And: “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.”

Assessment: Same proposal, scope, and retained routes. “Our” uses the representative author's collective organizational voice. The source supplies both the association's proposal and Sanna's spokesperson role, supporting that ownership. It does not invent any additional named authors, membership count, or natural gender. The six-month qualification carries from the immediately preceding heading and opening proposal.

### 25. Proposed time recording

Draft: “During the trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route …”

Source: “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg …”

Assessment: Same actor, proposed action, per-route granularity, and trial timing. In an opinion article, “should” expresses the association's proposal and does not misreport “ska” as an action already scheduled or completed.

### 26. Proposed voluntary reasons

Draft: “… and ask users to volunteer their reasons for choosing it.”

Source: “… och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.”

Assessment: Preserves the request, voluntary participation, user subject, reasons, and alternative routes. “Their” refers to plural users and adds no natural gender; the source's “de” likewise has third-person plural reference without natural-gender specification. “It” is singular because each user's explanation is for the route they chose; the source likewise asks why they choose telephone or web. Neither version requires that a person use only one route throughout the trial.

### 27. Author's acceptance of digital booking

Draft: “I do not oppose digital booking.”

Source: “Sanna Eks ståndpunkt: … Hon motsätter sig inte digital bokning.” Also: “Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut.”

Assessment: Same person's supplied standpoint, converted from third person to the commissioned first-person author voice. “I” is first-person singular without a natural-gender assertion. The source's “Hon” refers to Sanna and conveys feminine natural gender; the English first person does not reproduce that feature but also does not contradict it.

### 28. Telephone booking does not prove inability

Draft: “A telephone booking is not proof that someone cannot use the web …”

Source: “Sanna Eks ståndpunkt: ‘Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben. Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.’ Detta får citeras eller refereras.”

Assessment: Faithful paraphrase of the expressly supplied position. Same event, proof limitation, uncertain ability, person, and web-use scope. “Someone” is singular, indefinite, and gender-neutral, matching the person/number and absence of specified natural gender in “någon.” It does not deny inability or infer ability from the telephone choice.

### 29. Reason to investigate continued use

Draft: “… it is a reason to find out why the telephone is still used.”

Source: “Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.” This follows: “Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben.”

Assessment: Same standpoint and reason for inquiry. The draft does not supply an invented answer or user motive. “It” refers to the telephone booking, not to the indefinite person in the previous clause, and has no natural-gender content. “Still” preserves “fortfarande.”

### 30. Cost heading

Draft: “The board must weigh the trial’s cost.”

Source: “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket. Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till.” Also: “Slutet ska namnge kommunstyrelsens möjliga beslut, med kostnadsosäkerheten bevarad.”

Assessment: Same board, trial cost, and need to decide its treatment. “Must” expresses the supplied normative requirement, not a legal obligation. No cost amount, payment source, or available appropriation is implied.

### 31. Association's limited claim about financing and costing

Draft: “Öppna beslut makes no claim to have funded the trial or estimated its cost.”

Source: “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket.” Context: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.”

Assessment: Preserves whose claim it is and its limited scope. The draft does not turn “makes no claim” into certainty that the trial has not been funded or costed by anyone. “Its” refers to the trial and has no natural-gender assertion.

### 32. Closing decision: postponement, uncertain cost, and six-month authorization

Draft: “The municipal executive board should postpone the permanent switch, assess the trial’s still-uncertain cost, and authorize six months with both routes.”

Source: “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.” Also: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.” And: “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket. Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till.” Boundary: “Slutet ska namnge kommunstyrelsens möjliga beslut, med kostnadsosäkerheten bevarad.”

Assessment: Supported advocacy naming the decision maker and possible action. “Still-uncertain” is supported expressly by the brief's cost-uncertainty boundary, not inferred solely from the association's lack of a claim. “Authorize” is the author's requested board decision; it does not claim authorization has occurred, that a legal duty exists, or that money is already available. The preceding body preserves the all-seven trial scope.

### 33. Closing decision after the trial

Draft: “It can then decide whether to remove, change, or retain a booking route.”

Source: “Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till. Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.” Context: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar. Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.”

Assessment: Preserves the board's possible later decision and all three options. “Can” retains possibility, rather than promising an outcome. “A booking route” corresponds to “en kanal” in the supplied telephone/web context and does not assume which route will be removed or retained. “It” is third-person singular with the municipal executive board as referent. Swedish “den” has grammatical common gender, not a person's natural gender; English “it” introduces no natural-gender change. “Then” preserves the decision sequence, with the draft explicitly advocating the trial before the permanent decision.

## Findings

### F1 — Source support: “digital experience” changes the unmeasured variable

Draft passage: “The report measures neither age, functional ability, nor digital experience.”

Source passage: “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.” The antecedent of “Den” is “Rapporten” in “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer.”

Difference: What is measured. “Digital experience” can mean the quantity or history of a person's exposure to digital systems; “digital vana” describes being accustomed to, or familiar with, their use. A negative measurement claim about one does not establish the same absence for the other. No supplied sentence makes the two variables interchangeable or separately rules out measurement of digital experience.

Test, source true / draft false: The pilot could have asked users how many years they had used digital systems, while asking nothing about their current familiarity or accustomedness. Counting years of experience would measure digital experience, yet would not measure digital vana: a person could retain a long history of use while being unfamiliar with present interfaces after a long interruption. Such a question is compatible with the supplied source, which excludes measurement of digital vana, age, and functional ability but does not exclude years of digital use. Thus the source's negative statement can hold while the draft's expanded negative statement fails.

Test, draft true / source false: Conversely, a hypothetical report could record current comfort/familiarity when booking online while recording no amount or history of digital experience. That would measure vana without measuring experience understood as accumulated exposure. This converse scenario is excluded as an account of the actual supplied report by “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.” Setting that direction aside does not remove the first, compatible case.

Smallest supported repair: Replace “digital experience” with “digital familiarity”: “The report measures neither age, functional ability, nor digital familiarity.” Preserve the report attribution, negation, and other two variables.

Classification: Source-support finding from meaning changed across languages outside quotation. It is not a translated-quotation finding or a general preference about style.

## Translation findings

No direct quotations appear in the draft, so no translated-quotation findings apply. Other cross-language claims and pronoun features were checked in the accounting above. F1 is the only source-support finding.

## Completion status

Complete: The full supplied draft and full supplied material have been read and compared, and the factual claims, attributed standpoints, headings, implications, and relevant pronoun features have been accounted for. No files other than this report were written or altered. Unresolved findings: F1, pending the suggested source-supported repair to “digital experience.”

Where saying so is useful, the delivery names the suppressed instruction beside the resolved configuration.\\n\\nTwo things are never inferred. Fictional policy debate, not legal advice or a real municipal process. Source language: Swedish.\\n\\n## Brief\\n\\nSkriv en debattartikel för invånarna i fiktiva Lervik. Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut. Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb. En tydlig, sakligt skarp text på ungefär 400 ord. Markdown med orienterande rubriker. Ingen kommersiell CTA.\\n\\n## Underlag\\n\\nKommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler. Under försöket gjordes 96 bokningar via webb och 24 via telefon. Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.\\n\\nTjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni föreslår att telefonbokning tas bort för alla sju lokaler från september. Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden. Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna. Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.\\n\\nÖppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar. Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb. Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket. Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till. Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.\\n\\nSanna Eks ståndpunkt: ”Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben. Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.” Detta får citeras eller refereras. Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna. Hon motsätter sig inte digital bokning.\\n\\n## Boundaries / judging\\n\\nInga lagkrav, protester, diskrimineringsfall, partipolitiska motiv eller besparingar är belagda. Siffror ska tillskrivas rapporten där de bär argumentet. Författaren ska kunna kritisera beslutsunderlaget skarpt utan falska motiv eller tandlös neutralisering. Slutet ska namnge kommunstyrelsens möjliga beslut, med kostnadsosäkerheten bevarad.\\n\"}}"}, {"type": "input_text", "text": "{\"i\":2,\"status\":\"fulfilled\",\"value\":{\"chunk_id\":\"d53f39\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000001292,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":3,\"output\":\"AGENTS.md\\n\"}}"}], "internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough": {"turn_id": "01a0f762-9023-73a2-9c72-fa73afaf5217", "create_time": 1790856837.732646}}}

{"session": "01a0f762-8e98-7203-8735-e330899decb3", "timestamp": "2026-10-01T12:14:24.760Z", "result": {"type": "custom_tool_call_output", "id": "ctco_01a0f763-23f8-7e52-a027-7f7ed07d4512", "call_id": "call_U6rp2NockOE7V75rDVmRKhdj", "output": [{"type": "input_text", "text": "Script completed\nWall time 0.6 seconds\nOutput:\n"}, {"type": "input_text", "text": "{\"chunk_id\":\"d5dca5\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.41293825,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":212,\"output\":\"Invocation read.\\n\\nBefore anything else, answer each Capability under `dependencies.capabilities`: say whether its `confirm` sentence is true of you, and where one is not, give its `how`, change nothing, and stop.\\n\\n`$LIBRARY` is `/private/var/folders/cs/vgp_x_353zd9frkjpysp7zdw0000gn/T/kntnt-native-session-bgcn6qjf/work/.agents/skills/kntnt/library`.\\n\\n```json\\n{\\n  \\\"ok\\\": true,\\n  \\\"path\\\": [],\\n  \\\"flags\\\": {\\n    \\\"--genre\\\": \\\"opinion\\\",\\n    \\\"--language\\\": \\\"en_US\\\",\\n    \\\"--output\\\": \\\"response\\\"\\n  },\\n  \\\"operands\\\": [\\n    \\\"source.md\\\"\\n  ],\\n  \\\"instruction\\\": null,\\n  \\\"dependencies\\\": {\\n    \\\"ok\\\": true,\\n    \\\"unsatisfied\\\": [],\\n    \\\"capabilities\\\": [\\n      {\\n        \\\"name\\\": \\\"subagents\\\",\\n        \\\"confirm\\\": \\\"you can spawn subagents that work in their own context window\\\",\\n        \\\"how\\\": \\\"run this skill in a harness that can spawn subagents\\\"\\n      }\\n    ]\\n  }\\n}\\n```\\n\"}"}], "internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough": {"turn_id": "01a0f762-9023-73a2-9c72-fa73afaf5217", "create_time": 1790856864.760675}}}
{"session": "01a0f762-8e98-7203-8735-e330899decb3", "timestamp": "2026-10-01T12:14:39.831Z", "call": {"type": "custom_tool_call", "id": "ctc_09c032c80bfd0f54016abe4ea49dc487d2a03ce15318400616", "status": "completed", "call_id": "call_cBN4NdWRvhPcsdKtVNNxMGBv", "name": "exec", "input": "const results = await Promise.allSettled([\n  tools.exec_command({cmd:\"cat AGENTS.md .agents/skills/kntnt/library/references/invocation-envelope.md .agents/skills/kntnt/library/references/delivery.md .agents/skills/kntnt/library/references/editorial/genres/opinion.md\",max_output_tokens:22000}),\n  tools.exec_command({cmd:\"cat .agents/skills/write/references/source-check.md .agents/skills/write/references/quotations.md\",max_output_tokens:22000}),\n  tools.exec_command({cmd:\"rg --files .agents/skills/kntnt/library/references/editorial/techniques .agents/skills/kntnt/library/references/languages\",max_output_tokens:6000}),\n  tools.exec_command({cmd:\"python3 - <<'PY'\\nimport os\\nimport subprocess\\nimport tempfile\\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix='write-language-', dir='/private/var/folders/cs/vgp_x_353zd9frkjpysp7zdw0000gn/T/kntnt-native-session-bgcn6qjf/scratch') as task_tmp:\\n    env = dict(os.environ, TMPDIR=task_tmp, UV_NO_CACHE='1', UV_NO_PROJECT='1')\\n    result = subprocess.run(['uv', 'run', '--no-cache', '--no-project', '/private/var/folders/cs/vgp_x_353zd9frkjpysp7zdw0000gn/T/kntnt-native-session-bgcn6qjf/work/.agents/skills/kntnt/library/scripts/languages.py', 'resolve', '--scope=composition', 'en_US'], env=env)\\nraise SystemExit(result.returncode)\\nPY\",max_output_tokens:6000})\n]);\nfor (let i=0;i<results.length;i++) text({i,...results[i]});\n", "internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough": {"turn_id": "01a0f762-9023-73a2-9c72-fa73afaf5217", "create_time": 1790856865.250264}}}
{"session": "01a0f762-8e98-7203-8735-e330899decb3", "timestamp": "2026-10-01T12:14:40.321Z", "result": {"type": "custom_tool_call_output", "id": "ctco_01a0f763-60c1-78b1-994c-cdce70b60bb5", "call_id": "call_cBN4NdWRvhPcsdKtVNNxMGBv", "output": [{"type": "input_text", "text": "Script completed\nWall time 0.5 seconds\nOutput:\n"}, {"type": "input_text", "text": "Warning: truncated output (original token count: 11262)\nTotal output lines: 4\n\n{\"i\":0,\"status\":\"fulfilled\",\"value\":{\"chunk_id\":\"0a056d\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000001958,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":6171,\"output\":\"# Evaluation harness dispatch\\n\\nFor a `/write` or `/redline` invocation, read and execute the corresponding `.agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` in this project. These are the installed Skills for this invocation. Follow their shipped instructions.\\n\\nThe separate harness scratch area is `/private/var/folders/cs/vgp_x_353zd9frkjpysp7zdw0000gn/T/kntnt-native-session-bgcn6qjf/scratch`. Its existence does not authorize a Skill to leave files behind.\\n# The Invocation Envelope\\n\\nThe contract every Skill of this collection meets before it reads an argument of its own: how the reserved separator splits an Invocation Envelope, what a Contextual Instruction may and may not settle, the two refusals a bad Envelope or a bad Formal Invocation takes, and how a caller recovers from its own construction error. It is stated here once and executed by every Skill. A Skill's body follows this file, and its manpages point a reader at it; none of them restates it.\\n\\n## The separator\\n\\n`[**--** *INSTRUCTION*]` introduces an optional Contextual Instruction after the formal input. The first standalone, unquoted `--` token is the reserved separator; everything before it remains Formal Invocation and everything after it is instruction, including later `--` tokens. The instruction may start on the same line or after blank lines and must contain non-whitespace text. Attached or quoted forms such as `--force`, `foo--bar`, `` `--` ``, and `\\\"--\\\"` remain formal data. Without the separator, the complete payload remains formal input, including later lines and paragraphs.\\n\\nThe split happens before help routing and before formal validation, and only the Formal Invocation reaches Help, the Skill's own argument grammar, its scripts, and any nested formal parser.\\n\\n## Quoting a value\\n\\nAn operand and a flag value each arrive without the outer quotes they were written in — `\\\"a b\\\"` and `'a b'` are both the value `a b` — the pair being the caller's way of holding spaces together rather than part of what was written. Only a value whose own quoted run closes at its last character loses that pair, so an inner quote is kept (`'say \\\"hi\\\"'` is `say \\\"hi\\\"`), and a value that is two runs beside each other, a run closed early, a backtick-quoted run, or an unbalanced quote keeps every mark it carries. This happens after the split above, which it never moves: `\\\"--\\\"` is formal data exactly as stated there, and is the value `--`.\\n\\n## What a Contextual Instruction may settle\\n\\nA Contextual Instruction is read and used as natural-language guidance after the Formal Invocation is valid. Redundant but applicable guidance is valid. It may clarify or narrow choices the Skill leaves open and overrides older preferences within those choices, but cannot contradict formal input or an invariant, widen the Skill, disable a required gate, or request work outside its contract. Applicable guidance from Conversation Context has the same boundaries and need not be copied into the Invocation Envelope.\\n\\n## The two refusals\\n\\nMalformed formal input or an empty instruction takes the syntax refusal. The Skill names the error, prints the addressed SYNOPSIS, changes nothing, and points to help. Context on an exact help route takes the context refusal without rendering the page.\\n\\nValid but irrelevant, unaddressable, materially ambiguous, conflicting, or scope-widening guidance takes the distinct context refusal. The Skill names the guidance and its boundary, reports the mutation outcome, prints no synopsis, and stops without applying a valid remainder.\\n\\nUnaddressable guidance can affect nothing inside the Skill's contract. Guidance settled by a documented precedence is suppressed instead: the run continues and reports the suppression where useful. Suppression for one parameter does not invalidate guidance that applies to another.\\n\\nBefore the first side effect, the Skill uses available read-only checks to identify unusable guidance. If a conflict appears only after a legitimate effect, it stops before the next effect and reports the exact partial outcome. It rolls nothing back unless atomic behaviour was promised.\\n\\nThe addressed page is the most specific recognized command page — the Skill's own `help.md` where no command path was recognized — and the refusal points at that page's own `--help` route rather than at the Skill's root route.\\n\\nThe engine refuses every invalid form and never repairs or ignores one. When the user supplied the invalid form, the caller reports that refusal without repairing, reordering, discarding, or reinterpreting the user's syntax. A flag is refused rather than ignored where it has no work to do, an operand written before a flag is refused rather than reordered, and an incomplete form is refused rather than asked about — because a flag accepted and ignored teaches that flags sometimes do nothing, a form silently repaired teaches an order the Skill does not accept, and a question asked in place of the grammar leaves the user guessing at what the grammar is.\\n\\n## A caller's construction error\\n\\nWhen the caller introduced a known error while forwarding valid user input or constructing a nested invocation, and knows a valid correction that preserves the user's request and authority, it corrects its construction, submits the corrected invocation through the normal validation path, and continues from the failed boundary. The corrected construction preserves every resolved option, the complete artifact, and the authorised destination. Completed work is retained: a refusal before the called operation starts spends no substantive correction, budget round, or actual pass.\\n\\nRecovery requires that diagnosis and valid correction. An exit status alone never authorises a retry. Exact help, invalid user input, an unmet dependency, an unrelated failure, and a failure whose origin or valid correction is unknown keep their applicable handling; the caller reports the actual blocker instead of guessing or repeating the same failed call.\\n\\nBefore resuming an operation that may already have produced effects, the caller establishes what completed and resumes only what remains. It never replays a completed external action, widens permissions or the task, changes the authorised destination, or skips validation. Where the partial outcome cannot be established, it reports that uncertainty and stops.\\n\\n## A nested Skill\\n\\nA nested Skill receives only relevant guidance through an explicit Contextual Instruction in that Skill's own Invocation Envelope; an outer instruction is never forwarded blindly. Successful execution requires no context acknowledgement; an existing report names a materially changed choice where useful.\\n\\n## Worked cases\\n\\nThe following schematic cases pin the split independently of any one Skill's Formal Invocation grammar; `\\\\n\\\\n` denotes two newline characters in one payload.\\n\\n| Case | Envelope | Formal Invocation | Contextual Instruction | Outcome |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| Same line | `/skill --force -- Preserve deployment facts` | `/skill --force` | `Preserve deployment facts` | Envelope valid; formal grammar next |\\n| Blank lines | `/skill --force --\\\\n\\\\nPreserve deployment facts` | `/skill --force` | `Preserve deployment facts` | Envelope valid; formal grammar next |\\n| Empty suffix | `/skill --force --   ` | `/skill --force` | — | Syntax refusal |\\n| Later separator | `/skill -- Preserve -- deployment facts` | `/skill` | `Preserve -- deployment facts` | Envelope valid; formal grammar next |\\n| No separator | `/skill Preserve deployment facts` | `/skill Preserve deployment facts` | — | No split; formal grammar decides |\\n| Attached and quoted | ``/skill --force foo--bar `--` \\\"--\\\"`` | ``/skill --force foo--bar `--` \\\"--\\\"`` | — | No split; formal grammar decides |\\n| Exact help | `/skill --help -- Explain this page` | `/skill --help` | `Explain this page` | Context refusal; render nothing |\\n# Delivery\\n\\nEvery Skill that produces a Text Artifact delivers it the same way. This document is the complete contract: where a result goes, how a response carries the text, what the response carries when it went somewhere else, when a source file may be replaced instead, what happens when nothing changed, what language a run's own words about the text are written in and whether what those words say is true of the text they name, and what is refused before anything is written. It is a reference several Skills read rather than a runtime of its own. Each Skill exposes these rules through its own Formal Invocation and owns the names it gives them, so nothing here fixes how an option is spelled; what is fixed is the behaviour behind it.\\n\\n## The response is the default\\n\\nThe default Output Target is the agent response. A run that keeps the default delivers the complete Text Artifact in the response and changes nothing on the filesystem: it creates no file, touches no file, and makes no directory. That includes scratch files and working copies of the artifact, wherever the Harness says temporary files belong.\\n\\nA Harness convention identifies where an authorized temporary file belongs; it does not authorize a write. If a run genuinely needs a scratch file while composing, it removes that file before delivery and restores the filesystem to the state in which it found it. Before reporting delivery, the run checks the filesystem locations it used, including the Harness scratch area, and never reports that nothing was written while a copy of the artifact remains on disk.\\n\\nAn Output Target is independent of where the source material came from. Supplying a local file as source material selects no destination, and a run that reads a file still delivers to the response until the caller names somewhere else. Persisting a result is the caller's explicit choice, and making that choice is the whole of the authorization for it.\\n\\n## How a response carries the text\\n\\nA response carries a Text Artifact inside a fenced code block rather than as prose of the reply's own. A text and the words about it are two different things, and a reply that runs them together hands the reader a text nothing marks the edges of. Markdown is where that costs most: an artifact opening on a frontmatter delimiter, set directly beneath a paragraph the run wrote, turns that paragraph into a heading unless a blank line goes in between them — and a blank line there is what ends the frontmatter block the text was settled with. A fence removes the question rather than leaving each run to answer it, and the answers a run improvises are what made one saved reply in sixteen deliver a horizontal rule and a paragraph of configuration in place of a frontmatter block.\\n\\nThe complete Text Artifact sits inside exactly one fenced code block, byte for byte as the run settled it: frontmatter, blank lines and trailing structure included. Nothing is added, dropped or re-wrapped between the fence lines, and one block rather than several, a text split across two fences being a text the reader has to reassemble.\\n\\nThe fence is longer than the longest run of backticks inside the artifact, so a text carrying fenced code of its own cannot close it early. The info string names the text's format: `markdown` for a Markdown text, `html` for HTML, `text` for plain text.\\n\\nEverything the run says *about* the text sits outside the fence — the resolved configuration, the findings and which of them are unresolved, the claim account, where a technique came from, what the run could not do. Nothing of the run's own is inside it. What the run settled as part of the document does belong inside, the Handoff Metadata and any marking the user asked to have placed in the document itself among it, because that is the text as delivered rather than a remark about it.\\n\\nThe rule reaches every complete text a reply carries, whether this contract calls that text delivered or not. A response-targeted run that changed nothing and has findings left fences the artifact like any other; a run that stopped before it could deliver and carries its preserved prose in the reply fences that prose, and says in its own words outside the fence what the prose has not been through. The short no-change status carries no text at all and so carries no fence. An explicit destination and In-place Editing receive the text itself and never a fence: a fence is how a reply holds a text apart from its own words, and a file has no words of its own to be held apart from.\\n\\n## An explicit destination\\n\\nAn explicit destination is one filesystem path, and what exists at that path decides what happens to it.\\n\\n**A path that does not exist** creates exactly that one file and writes the complete Text Artifact into it. Exactly one file: nothing else is created beside it, and no directory is made to hold it.\\n\\n**A path naming an existing file** overwrites that exact file with the complete Text Artifact. No confirmation and no separate force option is required, and none is offered. Naming the path is the authorization, and demanding a second gesture on top of it would teach that naming a destination means something less than it says.\\n\\n**A path naming an existing directory** delivers into that directory under a filename the run derives, by the rules below. Choosing a directory selects a place, not a file, so it never overwrites a file already in it.\\n\\n## Deriving a filename for a directory\\n\\nA directory destination needs a name, and the name comes from what the run already knows. Take the first of these the run has: the basename of the local file that supplied the Text Artifact, the title of a referenced URL, then the Skill's own working title for the text it created. Reduce it to a name the filesystem accepts, and keep a suitable text extension — the source file's own where it had one, and otherwise the one matching the text's format.\\n\\nThat gives a stem and an extension, and the stem stays as it is for every delivery into that directory. Where nothing exists at the derived name, that name is the file written. Where something exists there, the existing file is never overwritten: keep the original stem and take the first free numbered candidate in ascending order, beginning at `-2`. So `my-file.md`, `my-file-2.md`, `my-file-3.md` are one sequence, growing by one name at a time.\\n\\nA numbered candidate is never adopted as the new stem. A third delivery into a directory already holding the first two is `my-file-3.md` and never `my-file-2-2.md`, whose stem would fork the sequence in two and leave the directory recording how many times the same text arrived rather than which arrival it was. Ascending order with the first free name also means a number freed by a deleted file is used again before a higher one is taken.\\n\\n## What the response carries when a destination was named\\n\\nA run that delivers to an explicit destination does not repeat the Text Artifact in the response. The response says where the text went — the derived filename included, where the destination was a directory — and carries what the file cannot: the findings the run reports beside the text, and which of them are unresolved. Naming a destination is saying where the text goes, and printing it again spends output on what the caller is already holding, doubly so when the file just written holds it too.\\n\\nThat holds whether or not the pass changed anything. A run that writes a file byte-identical to the source it read reports the destination and says the text needed no work, and does not become the one run that echoes a text the caller had before it started. The response is the default target precisely because a run that names no destination has nowhere else to put the artifact; naming one moves the text out of the response rather than duplicating it there.\\n\\n## In-place Editing\\n\\nIn-place Editing replaces the single local file that supplied the Text Artifact with the result, instead of delivering it anywhere else. It is available only to a Skill whose work is to return a changed version of a text the caller already has.\\n\\nA Skill that creates a new Text Artifact never edits its own source material in place. The brief, the interview, the notes, and the article it draws on are where the text came from and not where it goes; a Skill that could overwrite them would make supplying a file a risk rather than a convenience, and would lose the material the result is answerable to.\\n\\nIn-place Editing requires exactly one writable local source file, and it is mutually exclusive with a separate output option. A run replaces its source or delivers elsewhere, never both, and an invocation asking for both has named two destinations for one text.\\n\\nIt is refused wherever there is no such file to replace. Text supplied inline in the invocation or taken from the conversation is not a file. A URL is not a local file, and fetching one grants no right to write anything back. An uploaded source and a read-only file cannot be replaced. More than one Text Artifact leaves the single file the contract is written around undefined.\\n\\nAn explicit output path equal to the input path is refused in favour of In-place Editing, and the refusal says which gesture to use. Replacing a source then has one recognizable authorization: a reader of an invocation sees that a source is about to be replaced from the request itself, without comparing two paths to discover it.\\n\\n## When nothing changed\\n\\nA run may finish with nothing to change, and what it does then follows its destination.\\n\\nA response-targeted run that changed nothing returns a short no-change status in place of the text when no unresolved findings remain. When findings remain, deliver the complete Text Artifact in the response and report the unresolved findings separately.\\n\\nAn in-place run that changed nothing leaves the source file untouched and returns the short status with any unresolved findings. Rewriting a file with its own contents would make its timestamp claim that something happened.\\n\\nThat status is written in the language of the Text Artifact rather than the language of the invocation, so a Swedish text that needed no work is reported on in Swedish.\\n\\nAn explicitly selected different file or directory still receives the complete Text Artifact when nothing changed. Creating that artifact is what was asked for, and a destination left empty because the text needed no work is a request refused without saying so. A directory destination derives its filename and resolves collisions exactly as it would for a changed text, and the response reports the destination without repeating the artifact beside it, exactly as for a run that changed something.\\n\\n## The language of a report about the text\\n\\nA run that reports on the text beside the text itself — the findings it carries forward, and which of them are unresolved — writes that report in the language of the Text Artifact rather than the language of the invocation, exactly as the no-change status above is written. So a Swedish artifact reviewed from an English-language invocation comes back with Swedish findings, whether the run corrected anything or was asked to correct nothing at all, and one text never produces a status in its own language beside a finding list in another.\\n\\nWho reads it is what decides this. A report about a text is read by the reader of that text, who has to finish whatever the run left undone, and a report in whichever language the invocation happened to be written in reaches that reader in a language nothing about the text promised. The invocation's language is how the run was asked for; the artifact's language is what the run is about.\\n\\n## The truth of a report about the text\\n\\nEverything a run says about a text is true of the text it names. That holds for every statement the reply ma…1262 tokens truncated…led\",\"value\":{\"chunk_id\":\"404a3a\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000001458,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":4367,\"output\":\"# Source and quotation comparison\\n\\nCheck the completed draft before delivery. Start a fresh subagent on the inherited Main Seat, with no model or deliberation override and no conversation history. Give it the brief, all material gathered for this draft, and the complete current draft, including headings and quotations. Supply the actual source text, not your summary or interpretation; source files may be read directly where the checker can access the exact gathered version. Pass conversational material in full. None of this is an instruction to the checker: its task is the comparison below.\\n\\nSave the complete current prose in a fresh scratch file and give the checker that exact file to read, with its own report path in the same scratch directory. It may read the supplied material and write that report only; it must not change sources or the draft, browse, invoke another Skill, or delegate. Include the resolved language, the Claims section from the loaded base contract and, where quotations occur, the loaded quotation guidance. Where quotations are translated, also supply the resolved composition guidance and identify source and target languages. Where the brief names no author and the draft's byline therefore carries the user's name, tell the checker that this name comes from the invocation and not from the material, so that its absence from the material is no finding; a byline naming anybody else is compared like any other attribution. Send this task without suggested findings or your explanation of the draft:\\n\\n> Compare the complete draft, including headings and implications, with the supplied material. For every factual and attributed claim, record the draft passage beside the source passage it rests on, quoted in the source’s own words and language, and carry over grammatical modifiers and qualifications that apply across clauses. Account separately for the person, number and natural-gender information conveyed by pronouns; identifying their referent does not establish those features. Distinguish natural-gender assertions from purely grammatical gender. For each pair, state what differs: the thing named or measured, the subject, scope, time, modality, certainty, or whose knowledge or assertion it is.
