Reported counts, routes, duration, and setting\\n+\\n+Draft: “… records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial in two venues.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler. Under försöket gjordes 96 bokningar via webb och 24 via telefon.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Same counts, booking-event unit, routes, eight-week period, and two-premises scope. The draft supplies no percentage, comparative valuation, unique-user count, causal effect, or unsupported extrapolation.\\n+\\n+### 11. What the figures describe\\n+\\n+Draft: “Those figures describe booking activity.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Under försöket gjordes 96 bokningar via webb och 24 via telefon. Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Supported description of the counted unit. “Those” refers to the immediately preceding two counts; it adds no demographic information.\\n+\\n+### 12. Unknown number of people\\n+\\n+Draft: “They do not establish how many different people used either route.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Preserves the limitation of the counts. It does not claim that nobody used both routes, that the people are all different, or that there were no identifiable people. “They” refers to the figures, so its plurality does not assert anything about people or natural gender. “Different people” corresponds to unique persons. No particular unique-user number is invented.\\n+\\n+### 13. Unmeasured age\\n+\\n+Draft: “The report measures neither age …”\\n+\\n+Source: “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.” The referent of “Den” is established by the preceding sentence: “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Same report, negative measurement claim, and variable. The negation applies across all three coordinated variables in both languages. No age group or causal relationship to route choice is inferred.\\n+\\n+### 14. Unmeasured functional ability\\n+\\n+Draft: “… functional ability …”\\n+\\n+Source: “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: “Functional ability” expresses “funktionsförmåga” without narrowing it to a named disability, physical ability only, or a technical skill. The draft retains the report-level absence of measurement and adds no disability prevalence or exclusion claim.\\n+\\n+### 15. Unmeasured digital variable\\n+\\n+Draft: “… nor digital experience.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Source-support finding F1 below. “Digital experience” can name accumulated exposure or experience, whereas “digital vana” names accustomedness or familiarity with digital use. The negative measurement statement is not safely interchangeable between those variables.\\n+\\n+### 16. Limits of telephone-count inference\\n+\\n+Draft: “Turning the telephone total into a claim about residents unable to book online would misrepresent the evidence.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.” Also: “Sanna Eks ståndpunkt: ‘Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben. Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.’ Detta får citeras eller refereras.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Supported criticism of an inference drawn from the booking count. It neither denies that some residents could be unable to book online nor asserts that every telephone user is able to do so. Its broader wording about an inability claim is supported by Sanna's supplied position that an individual telephone booking is not proof of inability, alongside the report's population-inference limitation.\\n+\\n+### 17. Workload heading\\n+\\n+Draft: “The workload objection deserves a measured answer.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden. Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna. Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: The objection's existence and subject are supported. “Deserves” is the author's evaluation; “measured answer” is advocacy for the measurement supplied in the proposal. No workload amount or already demonstrated benefit is claimed.\\n+\\n+### 18. Administration's rationale\\n+\\n+Draft: “The administration wants staff to avoid entering information through two workflows.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden.” Also: “Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Same administration, staff activity, two-flow rationale, and desired avoidance. The source explicitly establishes the administration's actual objection, so “wants” does not manufacture an unstated motive. “Through” does not assert identical data-entry software or duplication of every field.\\n+\\n+### 19. Reality of the objection and judgment on it\\n+\\n+Draft: “That is a real objection to keeping both routes, and it deserves an answer.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.” Also: “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg …”\\n+\\n+Assessment: “Real” corresponds to the source's explicit “verkliga,” not an unsupported valuation of the size of the objection. “Deserves an answer” is the author's judgment grounded in the supplied objection and proposal. “That” refers to the preceding rationale, without any natural-gender content.\\n+\\n+### 20. Absence of time and savings calculations in the documents\\n+\\n+Draft: “But the documents contain neither a measurement of staff time nor a calculation of financial savings.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.” Context: “Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Same documents, absence claim, and two measures. “Staff time” identifies the subject established by the preceding staff-work rationale. The scope remains the documents; it does not claim that no person has measured time anywhere or that no savings could occur.\\n+\\n+### 21. Rationale versus quantified benefit\\n+\\n+Draft: “The board should not treat an administrative rationale as a quantified benefit.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden. Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Supported editorial argument from the distinction between a rationale and absent quantification. It does not assert that the board has already made this mistake or that the administrative rationale has no value.\\n+\\n+### 22. Staff cost and user value\\n+\\n+Draft: “Without those measurements, it cannot weigh staff costs against value to users.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.” Also: “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Supported expression of Sanna's reasoning and standpoint. It claims no staff-cost number and no amount of user value. “Those measurements” refers to the measurements absent from the documents; “it” refers to the board. The latter pronoun is singular, organizational, and does not convey natural gender. “Staff costs” corresponds to labor costs in this staff-administration context.\\n+\\n+### 23. Trial heading and prospective test\\n+\\n+Draft: “A six-month trial would test the trade-off.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar. Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.” Also: “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Supported prospective argument. “Would test” identifies the purpose of the proposed evidence collection; it does not guarantee a successful result, measured saving, or answer favoring either route. The duration remains six months.\\n+\\n+### 24. Scope, two routes, and first-person ownership\\n+\\n+Draft: “Our proposal covers all seven venues and retains telephone and web bookings.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut.” Also: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.” And: “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Same proposal, scope, and retained routes. “Our” uses the representative author's collective organizational voice. The source supplies both the association's proposal and Sanna's spokesperson role, supporting that ownership. It does not invent any additional named authors, membership count, or natural gender. The six-month qualification carries from the immediately preceding heading and opening proposal.\\n+\\n+### 25. Proposed time recording\\n+\\n+Draft: “During the trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route …”\\n+\\n+Source: “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg …”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Same actor, proposed action, per-route granularity, and trial timing. In an opinion article, “should” expresses the association's proposal and does not misreport “ska” as an action already scheduled or completed.\\n+\\n+### 26. Proposed voluntary reasons\\n+\\n+Draft: “… and ask users to volunteer their reasons for choosing it.”\\n+\\n+Source: “… och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Preserves the request, voluntary participation, user subject, reasons, and alternative routes. “Their” refers to plural users and adds no natural gender; the source's “de” likewise has third-person plural reference without natural-gender specification. “It” is singular because each user's explanation is for the route they chose; the source likewise asks why they choose telephone or web. Neither version requires that a person use only one route throughout the trial.\\n+\\n+### 27. Author's acceptance of digital booking\\n+\\n+Draft: “I do not oppose digital booking.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Sanna Eks ståndpunkt: … Hon motsätter sig inte digital bokning.” Also: “Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Same person's supplied standpoint, converted from third person to the commissioned first-person author voice. “I” is first-person singular without a natural-gender assertion. The source's “Hon” refers to Sanna and conveys feminine natural gender; the English first person does not reproduce that feature but also does not contradict it.\\n+\\n+### 28. Telephone booking does not prove inability\\n+\\n+Draft: “A telephone booking is not proof that someone cannot use the web …”\\n+\\n+Source: “Sanna Eks ståndpunkt: ‘Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben. Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.’ Detta får citeras eller refereras.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Faithful paraphrase of the expressly supplied position. Same event, proof limitation, uncertain ability, person, and web-use scope. “Someone” is singular, indefinite, and gender-neutral, matching the person/number and absence of specified natural gender in “någon.” It does not deny inability or infer ability from the telephone choice.\\n+\\n+### 29. Reason to investigate continued use\\n+\\n+Draft: “… it is a reason to find out why the telephone is still used.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.” This follows: “Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Same standpoint and reason for inquiry. The draft does not supply an invented answer or user motive. “It” refers to the telephone booking, not to the indefinite person in the previous clause, and has no natural-gender content. “Still” preserves “fortfarande.”\\n+\\n+### 30. Cost heading\\n+\\n+Draft: “The board must weigh the trial’s cost.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket. Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till.” Also: “Slutet ska namnge kommunstyrelsens möjliga beslut, med kostnadsosäkerheten bevarad.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Same board, trial cost, and need to decide its treatment. “Must” expresses the supplied normative requirement, not a legal obligation. No cost amount, payment source, or available appropriation is implied.\\n+\\n+### 31. Association's limited claim about financing and costing\\n+\\n+Draft: “Öppna beslut makes no claim to have funded the trial or estimated its cost.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket.” Context: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Preserves whose claim it is and its limited scope. The draft does not turn “makes no claim” into certainty that the trial has not been funded or costed by anyone. “Its” refers to the trial and has no natural-gender assertion.\\n+\\n+### 32. Closing decision: postponement, uncertain cost, and six-month authorization\\n+\\n+Draft: “The municipal executive board should postpone the permanent switch, assess the trial’s still-uncertain cost, and authorize six months with both routes.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.” Also: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.” And: “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket. Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till.” Boundary: “Slutet ska namnge kommunstyrelsens möjliga beslut, med kostnadsosäkerheten bevarad.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Supported advocacy naming the decision maker and possible action. “Still-uncertain” is supported expressly by the brief's cost-uncertainty boundary, not inferred solely from the association's lack of a claim. “Authorize” is the author's requested board decision; it does not claim authorization has occurred, that a legal duty exists, or that money is already available. The preceding body preserves the all-seven trial scope.\\n+\\n+### 33. Closing decision after the trial\\n+\\n+Draft: “It can then decide whether to remove, change, or retain a booking route.”\\n+\\n+Source: “Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till. Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.” Context: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar. Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.”\\n+\\n+Assessment: Preserves the board's possible later decision and all three options. “Can” retains possibility, rather than promising an outcome. “A booking route” corresponds to “en kanal” in the supplied telephone/web context and does not assume which route will be removed or retained. “It” is third-person singular with the municipal executive board as referent. Swedish “den” has grammatical common gender, not a person's natural gender; English “it” introduces no natural-gender change. “Then” preserves the decision sequence, with the draft explicitly advocating the trial before the permanent decision.\\n+\\n+## Findings\\n+\\n+### F1 — Source support: “digital experience” changes the unmeasured variable\\n+\\n+Draft passage: “The report measures neither age, functional ability, nor digital experience.”\\n+\\n+Source passage: “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.” The antecedent of “Den” is “Rapporten” in “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer.”\\n+\\n+Difference: What is measured. “Digital experience” can mean the quantity or history of a person's exposure to digital systems; “digital vana” describes being accustomed to, or familiar with, their use. A negative measurement claim about one does not establish the same absence for the other. No supplied sentence makes the two variables interchangeable or separately rules out measurement of digital experience.\\n+\\n+Test, source true / draft false: The pilot could have asked users how many years they had used digital systems, while asking nothing about their current familiarity or accustomedness. Counting years of experience would measure digital experience, yet would not measure digital vana: a person could retain a long history of use while being unfamiliar with present interfaces after a long interruption. Such a question is compatible with the supplied source, which excludes measurement of digital vana, age, and functional ability but does not exclude years of digital use. Thus the source's negative statement can hold while the draft's expanded negative statement fails.\\n+\\n+Test, draft true / source false: Conversely, a hypothetical report could record current comfort/familiarity when booking online while recording no amount or history of digital experience. That would measure vana without measuring experience understood as accumulated exposure. This converse scenario is excluded as an account of the actual supplied report by “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.” Setting that direction aside does not remove the first, compatible case.\\n+\\n+Smallest supported repair: Replace “digital experience” with “digital familiarity”: “The report measures neither age, functional ability, nor digital familiarity.” Preserve the report attribution, negation, and other two variables.\\n+\\n+Classification: Source-support finding from meaning changed across languages outside quotation. It is not a translated-quotation finding or a general preference about style.\\n+\\n+## Translation findings\\n+\\n+No direct quotations appear in the draft, so no translated-quotation findings apply. Other cross-language claims and pronoun features were checked in the accounting above. F1 is the only source-support finding.\\n+\\n+## Completion status\\n+\\n+Complete: The full supplied draft and full supplied material have been read and compared, and the factual claims, attributed standpoints, headings, implications, and relevant pronoun features have been accounted for. No files other than this report were written or altered. Unresolved findings: F1, pending the suggested source-supported repair to “digital experience.”\\n*** End Patch\"));\n", "internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough": {"turn_id": "01a0f76f-f062-7090-bc7a-221e417138db", "create_time": 1790857713.851337}}}
